# An <i>L</i><sup>3</sup> bound for the trilinear Hilbert transform

Coordinate `EN-110-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-C-ML-H-0190` | `PM-086.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `An-L3-bound-for-the-trilinear-Hilbert-transform-October-5-2026` | date 2026-10-05 | derived from the openai/math preprint An-L3-bound-for-the-trilinear-Hilbert-transform-October-5-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). {
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that the trilinear Hilbert transform with fixed slopes 1, 2, 3 maps $`L^3(\\mathbb R)\\times L^3(\\mathbb R)\\times L^3(\\mathbb R)`$ to $`L^1(\\mathbb R)`$, resolving this case of the trilinear Hilbert transform conjecture.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the trilinear Hilbert transform with fixed slopes 1, 2, 3 maps $`L^3(\mathbb R)\times L^3(\mathbb R)\times L^3(\mathbb R)`$ to $`L^1(\mathbb R)`$, resolving this case of the trilinear Hilbert transform conjecture.

- **claim-main-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a finite constant $C$ such that $$\lVert T(f_1,f_2,f_3)\rVert_{L^1(\mathbb R)} \le C\prod_{j=1}^3\lVert f_j\rVert_{L^3(\mathbb R)} \qquad(f_1,f_2,f_3\in\mathcal S(\mathbb R)).$$ Consequently $T$ has a unique bounded trilinear extension from $L^3(\mathbb R)\times L^3(\mathbb R)\times L^3(\mathbb R)$ to $L^1(\mathbb R)$.
  - Explanation: The trilinear Hilbert transform with slopes one, two and three is bounded from the product of three L^3 spaces into L^1 on Schwartz functions, with a unique bounded extension.
- **claim-local-absolute** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-absolute)): If $p_j\ge1$ and $\sum_{j=0}^3p_j^{-1}\le2$, then $$r^{-2}\int \|w_I(x,t)\|\prod_{j=0}^3\lVert z_j(x+jt)\rVert\,dx\,dt \lesssim\prod_{j=0}^3\lVert z_j\rVert_{p_j,I}.$$
  - Explanation: The local four-linear form with absolute value inside is controlled by the product of normalized local norms whenever the reciprocal exponents sum to at most two.
- **claim-uniform-local** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-local)): For some $q\in(2,3)$, the constants $C_N(q)$ are bounded independently of $N$.
  - Explanation: For some exponent in the open interval, the maximal-function constants for local form sums are bounded independently of the number of depths.
- **claim-normalized-absorption** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-normalized)): For a suitable fixed $q\in(2,3)$ and every sufficiently small fixed $c>0$, there is an $A<\infty$, independent of $N$, with the following property. Suppose a finite rooted subtree has root $I_*$, all summation cells lie in at most $N$ consecutive depths, and $$\lVert f_j\rVert_{q,I}\le1 \qquad(0\le j\le3, I\text{ in the subtree}).$$ Then $$\sum_{I\text{ a summation cell}}\|I\|\,\|H_I(f)\| \le (A+cC_N)\|I_*\|.$$
  - Explanation: Under local normalization on a finite rooted subtree, the sum of local forms is bounded by a constant plus a small multiple of the finite constant, times the root length.
- **claim-outer-reduction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-outer)): Proposition <a href="#prop:normalized" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:normalized">4</a> implies Theorem <a href="#thm:local" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:local">3</a>, and Theorem <a href="#thm:local" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:local">3</a> implies Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.
  - Explanation: The normalized absorption estimate gives the uniform local estimate, which in turn gives the main operator bound.
- **claim-attempt-packing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-attempt-packing)): Suppose a construction on a root $J$ sends exceptional cells and all their descendants to new estimates on disjoint subroots. Suppose that, at every root $R$, the new roots $R'$ satisfy $$\sum_{R'\text{ child root of }R}\|R'\|\le\rho \|R\|, \qquad 0\le\rho<1.$$ Then the sum of the lengths of all roots, including $J$, is at most $\|J\|/(1-\rho)$. In particular, if the contribution assigned to an attempt from $R$ is at most $A\|R\|$, with its hypotheses valid at every new root, the total contribution is at most $A\|J\|/(1-\rho)$.
  - Explanation: If child roots have geometric length fraction below one at each root, the total length of all roots is bounded by a geometric series.
- **claim-private-columns** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-private-columns)): If $\mathcal A$ is any set of columns available on a cell $I$, then $$D^{-1}\left(\sum_{v\in\mathcal A}\|c_v\|^2\right)^{1/2} \le \left\\|\sum_{v\in\mathcal A}c_v\Phi_v(y)\right\\|_{\mathcal H} \le D^{3/2}\left(\sum_{v\in\mathcal A}\|c_v\|^2\right)^{1/2} \quad\text{for almost every }y\in I.$$ In particular, the coefficients are unique. The orthogonal projection $P_{I,\mathcal A}$ onto their span satisfies $$\\|P_{I,\mathcal A}z\\|_{L^\infty(I;\mathcal H)} \le D^5\mathop{\mathchoice{\mkern 2mu\int\mkern-15mu-\mkern 7mu}{\mkern 2mu\int\mkern-13mu-\mkern 6mu}{\mkern 2mu\int\mkern-11mu-\mkern 5mu}{\mkern 2mu\int\mkern-9mu-\mkern 4mu}}\nolimits_I\\|z(y)\\|_{\mathcal H}\,dy.$$ For an actual scalar input $F=(f,0)$, write $P_{I,\mathcal A}F=\sum_{v\in\mathcal A}c_v\Phi_v$. Then $$\mathop{\mathchoice{\mkern 2mu\int\mkern-15mu-\mkern 7mu}{\mkern 2mu\int\mkern-13mu-\mkern 6mu}{\mkern 2mu\int\mkern-11mu-\mkern 5mu}{\mkern 2mu\int\mkern-9mu-\mkern 4mu}}\nolimits_I\bigl(f-(P_{I,\mathcal A}F)_{\mathrm{phys}}\bigr) \overline{\varphi_v} =\epsilon_v c_v \qquad(v\in\mathcal A).$$
  - Explanation: Private coordinates make column coefficients unique and well conditioned, and give a supremum bound for the local orthogonal projection.
- **claim-projection-prefixes** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-projection-prefixes)): Let $\mathcal X$ be a Hilbert space, and for $1\le i\le m$ let $P_0^i,\ldots,P_T^i$ be nested orthogonal projections on $\mathcal X$. Set $Z_n=P_n^m\cdots P_n^1$ and $$L_T=1+\left\lceil\log_2(T+1)\right\rceil.$$ Suppose $B_\nu:\mathcal X\to\mathcal Y_\nu$ are finitely many bounded linear operators satisfying $\sum_\nu\\|B_\nu h\\|^2\le\\|h\\|^2$. For any choices $n(\nu)\in\{0,\ldots,T\}$, $$\left(\sum_\nu\\|B_\nu Z_{n(\nu)}g\\|^2\right)^{1/2} \le L_T^m\\|g\\|.$$ Moreover, $$\sum_{n=1}^T\\|(Z_n-Z_{n-1})g\\|^2 \le m^2L_T^{2(m-1)}\\|g\\|^2 \le m^2L_T^{2m}\\|g\\|^2.$$ For $m=0$ the product is the identity and the first estimate has constant one.
  - Explanation: Products of nested prefix projections acting on row contractions cost only a power of a logarithm of the number of generations.
- **claim-compression** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-compression)): Use the column system and budget $D\ge2$ above on a finite dyadic tree with root $J$. Let $P^1,\ldots,P^m$ be inherited mask families, with their local orthogonal projections. Suppose finitely many bounded linear rows $$B_\nu:L^2(J;\mathcal H)\longrightarrow\mathcal Y_\nu$$ satisfy $$\sum_\nu\\|B_\nu z\\|_{\mathcal Y_\nu}^2\le\\|z\\|_{L^2(J;\mathcal H)}^2, \qquad B_\nu=B_\nu\mathbf 1_{u(\nu)},$$ where each $u(\nu)$ is a cell of the tree. Then an absolute constant $C$ satisfies $$\left(\sum_\nu \\|B_\nu P_{u(\nu)}^m\cdots P_{u(\nu)}^1z\\|^2\right)^{1/2} \le \bigl(C(m+1)^C\log(2D)\bigr)^{C(m+1)}\\|z\\|_{L^2(J;\mathcal H)}.$$ The estimate is uniform in the row cells, the total number of labels, and the number of spatial depths. Identity factors may be omitted. Products containing complements $1-P^i$ satisfy the same form of bound after changing $C$. Integrating such compositions against a fixed finite measure costs, in addition, at most the total variation of that measure.
  - Explanation: Row contractions composed with inherited local projection products are bounded in L^2 with a loss depending only on a logarithm of the budget and the number of families, uniformly in depth and label count.
- **claim-height-bins** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-height-bins)): Let $d\ge1$, $B\ge2$, and $\xi\in\mathbb R^d$. Let $$\mathcal E=\left\{\|a\cdot\xi\|: a\in\mathbb Q^d\text{ has height at most }B,\ a\cdot\xi\ne0\right\}.$$ There is an absolute constant $C$ such that the following assertions hold.* 1. *The set $\{\log_2v:v\in\mathcal E\}$ is contained in a union of at most $d$ intervals, each of length at most $C(d+1)^3\log(2B)$. In particular, $\mathcal E$ meets at most $C(d+1)^4\log(2B)$ dyadic magnitude bins.* 2. *If $r_n=r_0 2^{-n}$ with $r_0>0$, $\ell\in\{1,2\}$, and $0<\alpha\le\beta$, then $$\#\{n\in\mathbb Z:\text{ some }v\in\mathcal E \text{ satisfies }\alpha\le r_n^\ell v\le\beta\} \le C(d+1)^4\bigl(\log(2B)+\log(2\beta/\alpha)\bigr).$$* 3. *For every $\varepsilon>0$, with the maximum of an empty set defined to be zero, $$\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z} \max\bigl(\{r_n^\ell v:v\in\mathcal E, r_n^\ell v\le\varepsilon\}\cup\{0\}\bigr) \le C(d+1)^4\log(2B)\,\varepsilon.$$* *The same conclusions apply to suprema of finitely many such coordinate evaluations. For Euclidean norms of evaluation vectors with at most $h$ coordinates, one can add $C d\log(2h)$ to the bounds for the number of occupied bins and scales, and to the coefficient of $\varepsilon$ in the last estimate. No bound on the size of $\xi$ is required.
  - Explanation: Bounded-height rational combinations of a real frequency vector occupy only boundedly many logarithmic ranges, giving bounds on occupied bins and scales.
- **claim-chart-operations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chart-operations)): Complex conjugation, a product of a bounded number of atoms, and translation or affine rescaling of the real argument preserve the class <a href="#eq:src-1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-1">[eq:src-1]</a>, with a quasipolynomial change of complexity. The same statement holds for the multivariable charts introduced below. Only the unrestricted real oscillation parameters can depend on the size of the translation or rescaling.
  - Explanation: Conjugation, bounded products, translation and affine rescaling keep the class of quadratic chart atoms, with quasipolynomial complexity change.
- **claim-weyl-alternative** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weyl)): Let $\vartheta$ be supported in a fixed box in $\mathbb R^k$, with supremum norm and Lipschitz norm at most $H$. Suppose $0<\delta<1/2$ and $$\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb R^k} \left\|\int_{\mathbb R^k}\vartheta(z)Q(z)e(-\xi^Tz)\,dz\right\| \ge\delta .$$ There is a rational subspace $U\subset\mathbb R^d$, with orthogonal projections $P_f$ onto $U$ and $P_s=1-P_f$, such that $$\begin{gathered} A\|_{U\times U}=0,\qquad \\|V_s\\|\le H',\qquad \\|\Lambda_*\\|\le H',\\ V_f=P_fV,\quad V_s=P_sV,\qquad \Lambda_*=\Lambda+\tfrac12V^TC_{\mathrm{sy}}V+ \operatorname{sym}(V_f^TC_{\mathrm{sk}}V_s). \end{gathered} \tag{2}\label{eq:src-2}$$ The heights of $P_f,P_s$ are at most $H'$, where $$H'\le \exp\!\left(C(1+\log H+\log(1/\delta))^C\right).$$
  - Explanation: A large Fourier coefficient of a chart forces a rational splitting into an isotropic fast subspace and bounded slow and quadratic parts.
- **claim-chart-expansion** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-chart-expansion)): Suppose a rational orthogonal splitting satisfies <a href="#eq:src-2" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-2">[eq:src-2]</a>, with heights and the indicated sizes at most $B$. On a fixed box in $\mathbb R^k$ there is an exact absolutely convergent expansion $$Q(z)=e(z^T\Lambda_*z+b^Tz) \sum_\ell a_\ell e(\sigma_\ell^TVz). \tag{3}\label{eq:src-3}$$ It consists of quasipolynomially many families of Fourier series in at most $2d$ integer indices. Their rational frequencies have height at most $D(1+\\|\ell\\|)$, where $D$ is quasipolynomial in $H,B$. The total absolute coefficient sum is bounded by such a $D$.* *More quantitatively, for any fixed integer $J\ge1$, one can choose a quasipolynomial $D_J$ such that every dyadic shell of indices $T\le1+\\|\ell\\|<2T$ has absolute coefficient sum at most $$D_J T^{-J(d+1)^2}.$$ For fixed underlying oscillation parameters, the same estimate, after enlarging $D_J$, holds for the coefficient envelope obtained by pooling all rational projection choices of height at most $B$, separately for each resulting frequency and effective quadratic part. The coefficient envelopes are uniform over amplitudes with the stated complexity bounds and over the bounded slow values entering their construction. In particular the pooled tail can be made at most $\varepsilon$ by truncation at a size quasipolynomial in $H,B,\varepsilon^{-1}$.
  - Explanation: After the Weyl splitting a chart has an exact absolutely convergent Fourier expansion with polynomially decaying dyadic shell coefficient sums.
- **claim-ap-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ap-separation)): Let $\varphi_i,\varphi_k$ be bounded atoms of complexity at most $H$ in two distinct slots $i,k$, and let $a,b$ denote the remaining slots. Their product has an exact absolutely convergent expansion in terms $$c\,\psi_a(y_a)\psi_b(y_b)Q(t). \tag{4}\label{eq:src-4}$$ All three factors are bounded atoms independent of the interval scale. For some $D$ quasipolynomial in $H$, the terms can be arranged in dyadic shells $T\ge1$ with total coefficient mass at most $DT^{-100}$. In a shell $T$, each factor has dimension at most $\log D$, and complexity at most $(DT)^C$; unrestricted real linear phases are permitted as in Definition <a href="#def:quadratic-atom" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:quadratic-atom">11</a>.
  - Explanation: A product of two atoms in distinct slots along a progression expands into terms that separate into atoms in the remaining slots and in the progression parameter.
- **claim-single-scale-separation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-single-scale-separation)): Fix a term in <a href="#eq:src-4" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-4">[eq:src-4]</a>, without its coefficient, of complexity at most $H$. Let $\zeta$ be a fixed nonnegative smooth function, equal to one on the range of $t/r$ allowed by a local kernel $w_I$, and supported on a larger fixed interval. Put $$A_r=\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb R} \left\|\int_\mathbb RQ(ru)\zeta(u)e(-\xi u)\,du\right\|, \qquad r=\|I\|.$$ Its bilinear form norm in the remaining two $L^2(I)$ slots, with normalized interval norms, is at most $C A_r$.* *If $A_r\ge\delta>0$, then, for every $0<\varepsilon<1/2$, the kernel of this bilinear form, extended by zero off its support, can be approximated uniformly on $I\times I$, to error $\varepsilon$, by a finite separated sum $\sum_\nu c_\nu\phi_{\nu,a}(y_a)\phi_{\nu,b}(y_b)$. The $\phi_{\nu,e}$ are bounded atoms, and their complexities, their number, and $\sum_\nu\|c_\nu\|$ are quasipolynomial in $H,\delta^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1}$.* *Before separating the smooth kernel, the retained terms have phases $\lambda_*t^2+\gamma t$, where $\|\lambda_*\|r^2$ is quasipolynomially bounded. The quantities $\lambda_*$ are bounded-height rational combinations of $\lambda$ and the entries of $\theta\otimes\theta$; each $\gamma-\beta$ is a bounded-height rational combination of $\theta$. The height bounds are quasipolynomial in the same parameters.
  - Explanation: The single-scale bilinear form is controlled by a Fourier supremum, and when it is not small the kernel is approximated by separated sums of atoms.
- **claim-lss-inverse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-lss)): For a $1$-bounded function $f:[L]\to\mathbb C$ with $\\|f\\|_{U^3[L]}\ge\delta$, there are a degree-two filtered nilmanifold $G/\Gamma$, a polynomial sequence $g:\mathbb Z\to G$ adapted to its filtration, and a function $F:G/\Gamma\to\mathbb C$ such that $$\left\|\frac1L\sum_{n=1}^L f(n)\overline{F(g(n)\Gamma)}\right\|\ge\varepsilon.$$ The dimension is at most $C(1+\log(1/\delta))^{C}$, and the complexity of $G/\Gamma$, the Lipschitz norm of $F$, and $\varepsilon^{-1}$ are at most $\exp(C(1+\log(1/\delta))^C)$. The nilmanifold has a rational Malcev basis adapted to the filtration, with integer second-kind coordinates for $\Gamma$. The Lipschitz norm includes the supremum norm.
  - Explanation: A bounded function with large third Gowers norm correlates with a degree-two nilsequence of controlled dimension and complexity.
- **claim-cube-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cube-bounds)): The quantity $\\|\cdot\\|_{\mathcal U}$ is a seminorm. The mixed cube form with possibly different functions $g_\omega$ at its eight vertices satisfies $$\left\|\int_{\mathbb T_{16}^4} \prod_\omega\mathcal C^{\|\omega\|} g_\omega(x+\omega\cdot h)\,d\mu(x)\,d\mu(h)\right\| \le \prod_\omega\\|g_\omega\\|_{L^2(\mathbb T_{16})}.$$ Consequently cube forms are continuous in $L^2$, and $\\|g\\|_{\mathcal U}\le C\\|g\\|_{2,[0,1)}$ for supported functions.
  - Explanation: The continuous cube functional is a seminorm and mixed cube forms are bounded by products of L^2 norms.
- **claim-continuous-inverse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-continuous-inverse)): For $0<\delta<1/2$, let $g$ be supported on an interval $I$, with $\\|g\\|_\infty\le1$ and $\\|g\\|_{\mathcal U(I)}\ge\delta$. There is a bounded atom $\varphi$ of complexity at most $$H_\delta=\exp\!\left(C(1+\log(1/\delta))^C\right)$$ such that $$\left\|\mathop{\mathchoice{\mkern 2mu\int\mkern-15mu-\mkern 7mu}{\mkern 2mu\int\mkern-13mu-\mkern 6mu}{\mkern 2mu\int\mkern-11mu-\mkern 5mu}{\mkern 2mu\int\mkern-9mu-\mkern 4mu}}\nolimits_I g(y)\overline{\varphi(y)}\,dy\right\| \ge H_\delta^{-1}.$$
  - Explanation: A bounded function with large continuous cube seminorm correlates with a bounded atom of controlled complexity.
- **claim-bounded-decomposition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bounded-decomposition)): For $0<\tau<1/2$ and a function $f$ on $I$ with $\\|f\\|_\infty\le1$, there is a finite decomposition $$f=\sum_\nu c_\nu\varphi_\nu+u+v \quad\hbox{on }I$$ such that the $\varphi_\nu$ are bounded atoms of complexity at most $D_\tau$, $$\sum_\nu\|c_\nu\|\le D_\tau,\qquad \\|u\\|_{\mathcal U(I)}\le\tau,\qquad \\|v\\|_{1,I}\le\tau,$$ and $u,v\in L^2(I)$. Here $D_\tau\le\exp(C(1+\log(1/\tau))^C)$.
  - Explanation: A bounded function splits into a structured combination of atoms, a uniform part and a small L^1 part.
- **claim-local-von-neumann** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-von-neumann)): For four distinct slots occupied by $R,g,b,c$, one has $$\|H_I(R,g,b,c)\| \le C\\|R\\|_{2,I}\\|g\\|_{\mathcal U(I)} \\|b\\|_{\infty,I}\\|c\\|_{\infty,I}.$$ One also has $$\|H_I(R,g,b,c)\| \le C\\|R\\|_{1,I}\\|g\\|_{1,I} \\|b\\|_{\infty,I}\\|c\\|_{\infty,I}$$ for $g\in L^1(I)$, with the first estimate interpreted for $R,g\in L^2(I)$.
  - Explanation: The local four-linear form is bounded by an L^2 norm, a cube seminorm and two supremum norms.
- **claim-local-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-detection)): Fix $q>2$ and $C_1\ge1$. Suppose that $R,h,u,v$ occupy the four slots in any order and satisfy $$\\|R\\|_{2,I}\le C_1,\qquad \\|h\\|_{q,I}\le1,\qquad \\|u\\|_{2,I},\\|v\\|_{2,I}\le1,\qquad \|H_I(R,h,u,v)\|>\delta ,$$ where $0<\delta<1/2$. Then $R$ has a normalized moment against a bounded atom $\varphi$ of magnitude at least $D^{-1}$, where the complexity of $\varphi$ is at most $D$ and $$D\le \exp\!\left(C_q(1+\log C_1+\log(1/\delta))^C\right).$$ The absolute exponent $C$ is independent of $q$. The atom can be conjugated if the desired moment convention is bilinear rather than sesquilinear.
  - Explanation: A large local form forces a normalized moment of the L^2 input against a bounded atom of quasipolynomial complexity.
- **claim-low-scale-sum** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-low-scale-sum)): Suppose the inputs in positions $a,b$ are admissible sequences associated with $F_a,F_b$, with parameters $D_{\mathrm h},L$. For $m\ge0$, let $\mathcal I_m$ consist of intervals on which $e^{-m-1}<A_{\|I\|}\le e^{-m}$. Then $$\begin{split} &\sum_{I\in\mathcal I_m}\|I\| \left\|H_I\bigl(\psi_a z_{a,I},\psi_b z_{b,I};Q\bigr)\right\|\\ &\qquad\le e^{-m}\operatorname{poly}\bigl(1+m,\log(2H), \log(2D_{\mathrm h}),1+L\bigr) \\|\mathbf 1_JF_a\\|_2\\|\mathbf 1_JF_b\\|_2. \end{split} \tag{5}\label{eq:src-5}$$ The notation on the left denotes the two-input integral whose kernel is $w_I(x,t)Q(t)$, with the displayed multipliers in their respective positions. All intervals are contained in $J$. If $A_{\|I\|}=0$, the corresponding two-input integral vanishes.
  - Explanation: For a separated term, the scale sum over intervals in a band of the Fourier supremum decays exponentially in the band index up to polynomial losses.
- **claim-predictor-list** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-predictor-list)): Fix the two low atoms on $J$, with complexity at most $H_{\rm low}$. For every $B\ge1$, their product in the form can be replaced, with absolute length-weighted scale-sum error at most $$e^{-B}\operatorname{poly}\bigl(\log(2D_{\mathrm h}),1+L\bigr) \\|\mathbf 1_JF_a\\|_2\\|\mathbf 1_JF_b\\|_2,$$ by a finite list of terms $$\psi_a(y_a)\psi_b(y_b)w_I(x,t)e(\lambda_*t^2), \qquad \|\lambda_*\|\|I\|^2\le D_{\rm pred}. \tag{6}\label{eq:src-6}$$ The scalar coefficients may depend on the scale. The list of triples $(\psi_a,\psi_b,\lambda_*)$ is fixed on $J$. Its size, the absolute coefficient bounds, and all atom complexities satisfy $$\log D_{\rm pred} \le\operatorname{poly}\bigl(1+B,\log(2H_{\rm low})\bigr).$$ The multipliers $\psi_a,\psi_b$ may be normalized to have supremum norm at most one by incorporating their sizes into the coefficients.
  - Explanation: The product of two low atoms is replaced by a finite fixed list of quadratic terms with exponentially small scale-sum error.
- **claim-predictor-replacement** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-predictor-replacement)): Consider one fixed term of <a href="#eq:src-6" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-6">[eq:src-6]</a>, born on $J$. Suppose the nested predictor family in position $a$ contains $\Phi_p$ on every use interval and on all its descendants. Other columns are allowed, subject to the projection budget $D_{\mathrm h}$. Let the other position $b$ contain an admissible sequence. For a use interval $I$ of depth $s$, replace $F_a$ by the projection of $F_a$ on the partition at depth $s+K_0$, where $K_0\ge0$ is an integer. The absolute length-weighted error, summed over any collection of uses, is at most $$C\bigl(2^{-K_0}+(1+K_0)\sqrt\epsilon\bigr) \operatorname{poly}\bigl(D_{\rm pred},\log(2D_{\mathrm h}),1+L\bigr) \\|\mathbf 1_JF_a\\|_2\\|\mathbf 1_JF_b\\|_2. \tag{7}\label{eq:src-7}$$ The assertion also permits sequential replacement in positions $a,b$.
  - Explanation: Replacing an input by its projection on a delayed partition costs a smooth-approximation term and a private-coordinate term.
- **claim-adaptive-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-adaptive-approximation)): Suppose $\\|f_j\\|_{q,I}\le1$ throughout a finite normalized subtree. For every sufficiently large $k$, one can construct, on segments whose roots have bounded total length, local projections $g_I=P_IF_j$ such that $$\mathfrak d_{j,I}\bigl(f_j-(g_I)_{\mathrm{phys}}\bigr)\le e^{-k}, \qquad \\|g_I\\|_{2,I}\le1.$$ Within each segment the columns are inherited and the projections are nested in depth. Column path counts, atom complexities, and the physical supremum bounds of $g_I$ are at most $\exp(k^{O(1)})$. Fresh columns may all have private weight one. The polynomial degrees are independent of $q$.
  - Explanation: Inherited nested local projections make the tested physical residual exponentially small on segments whose roots have bounded total length.
- **claim-two-accuracy-expansion** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-two-accuracy-expansion)): Take the two earliest increment levels $u<t$ in distinct positions $i,j$, and let $a,b$ be the complementary positions. Define $$X_i=g_u-g_{u-4},\qquad X_j=g_t-g_{t-4},\qquad X_a=F_a-g_{a,\mathrm{prev}},\qquad X_b=F_b-g_{b,\mathrm{prev}}. \tag{8}\label{eq:src-8}$$ Here $g_{e,\mathrm{prev}}$ is the last update in position $e$ before level $t$, and is zero if no such update exists. For a finite collection of intervals, the original form is the limit of the sum of the corresponding tuple forms $H_I(X)$ as the update cutoff tends to infinity. If $A$ is sufficiently large, there is an absolute $\theta_0>0$ such that, whenever $t\ge12$ and $k=k_t$, every pair of full arguments in <a href="#eq:src-8" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-8">[eq:src-8]</a> has bilinear form norm at most $e^{-2k^{\theta_0}}$ against two normalized $L^2(I)$ inputs. All four arguments have bounded normalized $L^2(I)$ size.
  - Explanation: Isolating the two earliest increments makes every pair of full arguments have very small bilinear form norm.
- **claim-high-truncation** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-high-truncation)): For the tuple above, put $k=k_t$. There are an integer $K_0\le k^{O(1)}$, further bounded-packing segments, and nested projection families $P^{\rm new}_a,P^{\rm new}_b$ on those segments, for which $$Y_i=X_i,\qquad Y_j=X_j,\qquad Y_e=P^{\rm new}_{e,s+K_0}F_e-g_{e,\mathrm{prev}}\quad(e=a,b), \qquad s=\operatorname{depth}(I). \tag{9}\label{eq:src-9}$$ The absolute length-weighted replacement error in $H_I$, summed over the original normalized root, is at most $Ce^{-k}$ times its length. Path counts, inverse private weights, atom complexities, and the physical supremum bounds on coarse evaluation cells are all at most $\exp(k^{O(1)})$. For $t\ge12$, after decreasing an absolute $\theta>0$ if necessary, every pair of full $Y$ arguments has form norm at most $\eta=e^{-k^\theta}$ against normalized $L^2$ opponents. All $Y$ arguments have bounded normalized $L^2$ size.
  - Explanation: The two high positions are truncated to delayed projections with small replacement error while pairs of full arguments stay small.
- **claim-active-triple** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-active-triple)): Fix three bounded basic scalar columns, in three distinct positions, on their common birth subtree. For a dyadic interval $I$ let $\mathcal A_I$ be the norm of their form against a normalized $L^2(I)$ input in the fourth position. Along every spatial path, the number of scales for which $\mathcal A_I>\delta$ is polynomial in $\log D_{\rm base}+\log(1/\delta)$. Moreover, for $0<\delta\le1$, $$\sum_{I\text{ on the path}:\,\mathcal A_I\le\delta} \mathcal A_I \le \delta\operatorname{poly} \bigl(\log D_{\rm base},\log(1/\delta)\bigr).$$ The bound is uniform in the locations of the path and in all unrestricted oscillation parameters.
  - Explanation: For a fixed triple of basic columns only polynomially many scales on a path are active, and small norms sum with a small total.
- **claim-base-reduction** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-base-reduction)): The remaining patterns, with at least three $B$ arguments and optionally one $U$ argument, can be restricted, with total absolute error at most $e^{-k}\|R\|$, to a common active collection whose path multiplicity satisfies $$M\le D_{\rm base}^{C}. \tag{10}\label{eq:src-10}$$ The finitely many leading update levels have a bound independent of the number of depths. For $t\ge12$, it suffices on each segment and for each retained pattern $z$ to estimate $$\sum_{I\ \mathrm{active}}\|I\| \min\bigl(\eta,\|H_I(z)\|\bigr). \tag{11}\label{eq:src-11}$$ The two nonside arguments complementary to a pair of later side arguments are the whole $B$ arguments of this tuple before further filters. Restoring them to their full $Y$ arguments recovers the pair-smallness above. Further attempts may restart on normalized subroots with these basic arguments and their inherited lists unchanged.
  - Explanation: Remaining patterns reduce, up to small error, to a common active collection with bounded path multiplicity and a minimum-with-eta cell sum.
- **claim-tag-separation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tag-separation)): Fix an upper bound $\exp(k^{O(1)})$ for the complexities of the single columns under consideration. Given $\tau_2>0$ with $\log(1/\tau_2)\le k^{O(1)}$, one can choose $H_{\rm ed}=\exp(k^{O(1)})$ so that the following statements hold. If $d_s(v,w)>1$, the physical inner product of two same-slot pure columns of these tags, normalized on any interval of depth $s$, has absolute value at most $\tau_2$. If the two columns are in distinct slots, their four-linear form has norm at most $\tau_2$ against two arbitrary normalized $L^2(I)$ arguments in the other slots.
  - Explanation: Columns whose curvature tags are far apart in the ambient graph are nearly orthogonal, and their four-linear form is small.
- **claim-curvature-path** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-curvature-path)): Suppose two tags can be joined, at one scale, by a path of length $L\le\operatorname{poly}(P,k)$. Their difference data satisfy <a href="#eq:src-2" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-2">[eq:src-2]</a> in their own endpoint coordinates, with $\log H'\le\operatorname{poly}(P,k)$.
  - Explanation: Tags joined by a short path have difference data satisfying the splitting alternative at endpoints with controlled height.
- **claim-color-budget** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-color-budget)): For sufficiently large starting $k$, the preceding construction uses at most $\mathcal L$ colors, with at most $d_0^8$ changes per vertex on a path. Delays can be chosen so that the first cells where a required primary or high marking fails have arbitrarily small fixed total length relative to $R$. They can be sent to fresh attempts. All statements below are on the remaining paths, where every needed marking exists.
  - Explanation: The coloring uses a bounded number of colors with few changes per vertex, and failures lie in exceptional roots of small length.
- **claim-historical-masks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-historical-masks)): The following assertions hold for every allowed pad.* 1. *At an actual use with current central vertex $v$, every present vertex $b$ with $d_s(b,v)\le h$ lies in $\mathcal M_{G,h}$.* 2. *At an actual use, every present graph neighbor of a vertex in $\mathcal M_{G,h}$ belongs to $\mathcal M_{G,h+1}$ if $h<H_{\max}$. Every vertex admitted to a mask has, already at admission, a graph path to $z$ of length at most $\operatorname{poly}(P,k)$.* 3. *For distinct groups of the same color, vertices belonging to their pad masks on overlapping usable endpoints are at graph distance greater than $1$ at the finer endpoint.
  - Explanation: Historical pad masks contain nearby vertices at actual uses, are closed under neighbors, and separate distinct groups of one color.
- **claim-usable-bessel** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-usable-bessel)): Fix a slot and a color. For each of finitely many groups $G$ let $Z_G$ be a finite sum of available main pure columns, with constant coefficients on *entire* usable endpoint cells whose pad masks contain their tags. Different terms may use arbitrary endpoint times, and a label may be used repeatedly. Suppose the number of groups and available column labels on a spatial path, and the inverse private weights, are bounded by $\exp(k^{O(1)})$. Choosing $\tau_2$ sufficiently small after these bounds gives $$\lVert \sum_G Z_G\rVert^2\lesssim\sum_G\lVert Z_G\rVert^2.$$ The constant has no dependence on the number of endpoint times or on the depth of a future use certificate.
  - Explanation: Sums over distinct groups of column combinations on entire usable cells are almost orthogonal once the separation tolerance is small enough.
- **claim-same-tag** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-same-tag)): For each original vertex $v$ there is a fixed template family in every slot, consisting of bounded pure columns of normalized tag $v$ and unrestricted linear modulations, with the following property. If a pure source column of tag $v$ in slot $i$ has form norm $t_0>0$ against a physical residual in slot $j$ and two normalized $L^2(I)$ spectators, then the residual has normalized moment at least $t_0/H_0$ against a template column of the same tag in slot $j$. Here $H_0\le\exp(k^{O(1)})$ is independent of all insertion ranks and of the linear modulations. The source can be either an original column or any column in these template families.
  - Explanation: A nontrivial form against a residual forces a quantified moment of the residual against a same-tag template column.
- **claim-catalog-closure** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-catalog-closure)): The main closure and then the helper closure can be performed at every active cell with path counts satisfying $\log n_{\max}\le k^{O(1)}$. The exponent is fixed independently of $H_{\rm ed}$ and of subsequent linearization precision. All fresh columns have the template complexities of Lemma <a href="#lem:same-tag" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:same-tag">36</a>.
  - Explanation: Main and then helper projection catalogs close within a budget whose logarithm is polynomial in the accuracy parameter.
- **claim-localization** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-localization)): For an active pattern in <a href="#eq:src-11" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-11">[eq:src-11]</a>, there are $\operatorname{poly}(k)$ fixed search leaves such that, up to total absolute length-weighted error at most $Ce^{-k}\|R\|$, each leaf contributes $$\sum_{G\ \mathrm{current}} H_I(Z_{0,G,s},Z_{1,G,s},Z_{2,G,s},Z_{3,G,s}),\qquad Z_{l,G,s}=\overline L_{l,G,s}\mathcal B_{l,s}z_l. \tag{14}\label{eq:src-14}$$ Here $\overline L_{l,G,s}$ is one positive smeared ball filter, and $\mathcal B_{l,s}$ is a product of cumulative filters or complements of length $O(\log\mathcal L)$, independent of $G$. The product and slot choices are fixed by the search leaf, uniformly over cells. A group is summed only at an actual current central use. The minimum with $\eta$ in <a href="#eq:src-11" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-11">[eq:src-11]</a> may be applied separately per leaf.
  - Explanation: Active patterns localize, up to small error, into sums over current groups across polynomially many search leaves.
- **claim-transfer** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-transfer)): At a use of <a href="#eq:src-14" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-14">[eq:src-14]</a>, suppose slots $a,b$ are fixed side states $S_a,S_b$, chosen independently of the helper catalog. Suppose the other two slots have whole arguments $Z_{c,G,s},Z_{d,G,s}$ with basic inputs $B_c,B_d$. Expand every factor $1-\overline Q$ in slots $c,d$ as the difference of the identity and a positive average. In each resulting term, all positive filters on these two slots can be removed, one at a time, by transferring corresponding helper averages to slot $a$. The main term at the end is $$H_I(\mathcal D_{a,s}S_a,S_b,B_c,B_d),$$ where $\mathcal D_{a,s}$ is a product of helper averages of length $O(\log\mathcal L)$.* *For a step transferring a filter from $c$ to $a$, let $Q_{c,p}$ be its individual outgoing projections, $1\le p\le P$, and let $Q_{a,p}$ be the helper-rank projections with the same masks and pads. For $l=a,c$, put $$Q_{l,0}=0,\qquad Q_{l,P+1}=1,\qquad A_l^p=Q_{l,p}-Q_{l,p-1},\qquad \overline Q_l=P^{-1}\sum_{p=1}^P Q_{l,p}.$$ Let $x_c$ be the inner history on $B_c$ after deleting the outgoing filter. In the other slots, let $x_a=\mathcal D_{a,s}S_a$ use the helper averages already transferred, let $x_b=S_b$, and let $x_d$ be the whole remaining history on $B_d$. The exact difference $$H_I(x_a,x_b,\overline Q_c x_c,x_d) -H_I(\overline Q_a x_a,x_b,x_c,x_d)$$ is $$\sum_{p,q=1}^{P+1}\frac{p-q}{P} H_I(A_a^p x_a,x_b,A_c^q x_c,x_d). \tag{15}\label{eq:src-15}$$ Only $\|p-q\|\le2$ need be retained. The discarded far terms at each cell and group have total absolute size at most $D^{-C_3}\lVert S_a\rVert_{2,I}\lVert S_b\rVert_{2,I}$, for any prescribed fixed $C_3$, after the indicated choices of numerical accuracies.
  - Explanation: Complementary filters in two slots are transferred to helper averages in a side slot, leaving near-band defects.
- **claim-mixed-maximal** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sides-mixed-maximal)): Let $(X,\mu)$ and $(W,\nu)$ be finite measure spaces. If $1<r,s,t<\infty$ and $v\in A_r(\mathbb R)$, then $$\\|M_y g\\|_{L_y^r(v;L_\xi^s(X;L_w^t(W)))} \le C_{r,s,t}([v]_{A_r}) \\|g\\|_{L_y^r(v;L_\xi^s(X;L_w^t(W)))}.$$ The constant is independent of the numbers of atoms in the measure spaces. In particular, for finite exponents $p,b,h>q\geq1$, $$\\|M_q f\\|_{L_y^pL_\xi^bL_w^h} \le C_{p,b,h,q}\\|f\\|_{L_y^pL_\xi^bL_w^h}.$$
  - Explanation: The maximal operator is bounded on iterated mixed Lebesgue norms with constants independent of the number of atoms.
- **claim-short-block** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-short-block)): Let $2<q<3$. Let $\mathcal T$ be a finite dyadic tree piece with root $R$, whose internal summation intervals belong to at most $K$ consecutive depths and to the original range of at most $N$ depths. Suppose its leaves occur in the same depth block or one depth below it. In slot $j\in\{0,1,2,3\}$ let $\mathbf z_j$ be a deterministic Hilbert-space-valued function supported on $R$, with $$\\|\mathbf z_j\\|_{2,L}\le s_j \qquad\text{for every leaf }L.$$ Let $z_j^\sigma$ be random scalar inputs such that, for every fixed finite $a\geq1$ used below, $$\bigl(\mathbb E_\sigma\|z_j^\sigma(y)\|^a\bigr)^{1/a} \le C_a\\|\mathbf z_j(y)\\|.$$ The constants $C_a$ are independent of the tree and $K$; products of at most two independent Rademacher randomizations of a vector stack have this property. For arbitrary measurable kernel signs $\|\epsilon_I(\sigma)\|\le1$, and any subcollection of the internal intervals, $$\mathbb E_\sigma\left\| \sum_I \|I\|\epsilon_I(\sigma) H_I(z_0^\sigma,z_1^\sigma,z_2^\sigma,z_3^\sigma) \right\| \le (1+C_N(q))C_q (1+\log(K+2))^C(K+2)^{C(q-2)} \|R\|\prod_{j=0}^3s_j . \tag{16}\label{eq:src-16}$$ Here $C$ is absolute. The constant $C_q$ may depend on the fixed moment bounds, as well as the fixed kernel bounds.
  - Explanation: A tree piece on few consecutive depths is controlled by the local constant times a mild power of the block length.
- **claim-sides-variation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sides-variation)): Let $Z_{G,s}$ be a basic coarse output in Equation <a href="#eq:src-14" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-14">[eq:src-14]</a>. Consider a common collection of disjoint, or boundedly overlapping, depth intervals $[r,t]$. At each right endpoint, restrict to a union of $t$-cells having a downstream actual use certificate for $G$. Then $$\sum_{G,[r,t]}\\| \mathbf 1_{E_{G,r,t}}(Z_{G,t}-Z_{G,r})\\|_2^2 \le G_0\\|\mathbf 1_RF\\|_2^2.$$ The same conclusion holds for a shared coarse history with no outer ball, omitting the group sum. The constant only acquires the given overlap multiplicity.
  - Explanation: Increments of a coarse output on certified endpoint cells have square sum bounded by the overhead factor times the input energy.
- **claim-sides-stacks** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sides-stacks)): Fix a near offset $q-p$ in Equation <a href="#eq:src-15" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-15">[eq:src-15]</a>, a transfer step, and a search path. The two time-dependent side inputs can be written, at their uses, as a sum of at most small-overhead many configurations of fixed vector stacks for each $(G,b)$. One position is selected at a use from each stack. The banded side stack also includes the band index. After absorbing the number of configurations in $G_0$, their energies satisfy $$\sum_{G,b}\\|\mathrm{stack}_{a,G,b}\\|_2^2 \le P G_0\|R\|, \qquad \sum_{G,b}\\|\mathrm{stack}_{\mathrm{untouched},G,b}\\|_2^2 \le G_0\|R\|. \tag{18}\label{eq:src-18}$$ The functions in these stacks are fixed before subsequent cuts of the summation set.
  - Explanation: Time-dependent side inputs are written as fixed vector stacks whose energies are bounded.
- **claim-side-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-side-estimate)): Assume the base and localization hypotheses above, the helper conclusions of Lemma <a href="#lem:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:transfer">39</a>, and the linear-side square bound <a href="#eq:src-17" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-17">[eq:src-17]</a>. Fix $p\in(2/3,1)$. After common first-crossing cuts of arbitrarily small fixed total relative length, terms having at least three side factors have total length-weighted $p$th-power cost at most $P^C\|R\|$, provided the fourth factor has retained normalized $L^2$ size at most $P^C$. This includes a fourth side, a bounded current argument, or a bounded current argument minus side factors.* *Terms having exactly two side factors and whole current coarse complements admit the following estimates. Their freeze errors, large-complement terms, and restoration errors have total length-weighted $p$th-power cost at most $P^C\|R\|$. Their transferred main terms cost at most $\eta P^C\|R\|$ absolutely. Their numerical errors are negligible after the stated choice of the main precision. Their remaining coupled near-band terms obey Equation <a href="#eq:src-19" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-19">[eq:src-19]</a> without taking a power less than one.* *All statements are uniform on normalized subroots with the same basic data. Subsequent common cuts may remove original summands without changing the fixed functions used in these estimates.
  - Explanation: Terms with three side factors are summable at a power, and two-side terms reduce to a small transferred main term plus near bands.
- **claim-first-match-lift** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-first-match-lift)): At each first match $I_0$, there is a finite, permanently labeled expansion $$\zeta^\rho(y)\varphi_\Phi(y)e(-c_l p^\rho(y)) =\sum_m a_{\Phi,m}e(\lambda_m y^2+\gamma_m y) +O(\delta_c),\qquad y\in I_0,$$ where all the modes belonging to this primary have the same $\lambda_m$. We may use augmented mode atoms $$\psi_m(y)= \left( \zeta^\rho(y)e(c_l p^\rho(y)) e(\lambda_m y^2+\gamma_m y), \ \epsilon_{\mathrm m}^{1/2}\mathbf e_m \right). \tag{20}\label{eq:src-20}$$ Their private indices distinguish primaries and first-match expansions. For an admissible budget $D_1$, with $\log D_1\le\operatorname{poly}(P,k)$, we have $$\|\lambda_m\|\,\|I_0\|^2\le D_1,\qquad \sum_m\|a_{\Phi,m}\|\le D_1,$$ and the number of modes is at most $D_1$. For two modes of this primary, $$\gamma_m-\gamma_{m'}=a\cdot(\theta_v,\vartheta), \qquad a\in\mathbb Q^{\dim(\theta_v,\vartheta)} \text{ has height at most }D_1 . \tag{21}\label{eq:src-21}$$ There is a block-independent pointwise linear operator $\mathscr R_{l,G}$, of uniformly bounded norm, mapping each frozen outer ball output to exactly the corresponding synthesis of Equation <a href="#eq:src-20" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-20">[eq:src-20]</a>.
  - Explanation: At the first match each primary has a labeled finite mode expansion and a bounded pointwise lift operator.
- **claim-linear-exceptional** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-linear-exceptional)): Each primary is exceptional in at most $C d_{\max}$ blocks on a path. At a nonexceptional block, let $K_{\mathrm{rat}}$ be the rational span of all height-$\mathcal H$ vectors satisfying the tiny alternative in Equation <a href="#eq:src-23" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-23">[eq:src-23]</a>. If a vector of height at most $\mathcal H$ belongs to $K_{\mathrm{rat}}$, then it too satisfies that tiny alternative.
  - Explanation: Each primary is exceptional in boundedly many blocks on a path, and the tiny rational space is closed under bounded-height membership.
- **claim-height-stabilization** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-height-stabilization)): For a nonexceptional primary, all but $C d_{\max}R_*^{\mathrm{ht}}$ heights have the following property: if two of its modes are in one CURRENT component at height $h$, then they are directly adjacent in the PRE graph at height $h-\Delta$. We call these heights height-good for the primary.
  - Explanation: For a nonexceptional primary, connectedness in the current graph implies direct adjacency in the earlier graph at all but few heights.
- **claim-good-predicates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-good-predicates)): Good modes in one CURRENT component at height $h$ have the same PRE component at height $h-\Delta$. The core/side decision is constant on each minimal PRE component. Moreover, core modes in one minimal CURRENT component have identical good predicates throughout $$\mathcal H_{\mathrm{up}} :=\{h\in\mathbb Z:\lceil4\delta_*J_*\rceil\le h\le J_*\},$$ and have the same PRE components at height $h-\Delta$ there. Every core mode has bad proportion at most $2\delta_*$ in $\mathcal H_{\mathrm{up}}$.
  - Explanation: Good modes in a current component share an earlier component, and core modes have a common good predicate on the upper height range.
- **claim-entry-rank** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-entry-rank)): Let $S_1\subseteq\cdots\subseteq S_T$ be nonempty finite sets with $\|S_T\|\le M$. Suppose $X_t\subseteq S_t$, and each element belongs to at most $A$ of the sets $X_t$. Then $$\sum_{t=1}^T\frac{\|X_t\|}{\|S_t\|} \le A H_M,\qquad H_M=\sum_{r=1}^{\lfloor M\rfloor}\frac1r\le1+\log M.$$
  - Explanation: Relative sizes of subsets in a nested chain sum to at most a harmonic number times the multiplicity.
- **claim-bad-density-chain** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-bad-density-chain)): Fix $0<\delta,\beta<1$, integers $J\ge\Delta\ge1$, and $M\ge1$. For $1\le i\le N$, $0\le h\le J$, let $\mathsf P_{i,h}$ and $\mathsf C_{i,h}$ be nonempty sets of primary labels, each increasing in $h$, with cardinality at most $M$, and satisfying $$\mathsf P_{i,h}\subseteq\mathsf C_{i,h} \subseteq\mathsf P_{i+1,h}\qquad(i<N).$$ Let $X_i$ be the exceptional primaries at position $i$, each primary exceptional at at most $B$ positions. For a fixed position, suppose each nonexceptional primary is height-bad at at most $A$ heights; write $Y_{i,h}$ for these height-bad primaries. Declare $h<\Delta$ bad, and for other heights declare $h$ bad if the floor logs of $\|\mathsf P_{i,h-\Delta}\|$ and $\|\mathsf C_{i,h}\|$, to base $1.1$, differ, or if $$\frac{\|(X_i\cup Y_{i,h})\cap\mathsf C_{i,h}\|} {\|\mathsf C_{i,h}\|}>\beta.$$ Suppose each position has more than $\delta(J+1)$ bad heights. Set $L=\lfloor\log_{1.1}M\rfloor$ and $$Q=\Delta(L+1)+\beta^{-1}AH_M.$$ If $J+1\ge2Q/\delta$, then $$N<\frac2\delta\bigl(L+\beta^{-1}BH_M\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Chains of nested primary sets with a high density of bad heights have bounded length.
- **claim-separated-mode-rows** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-separated-mode-rows)): Fix a role and group. Consider a family of dyadic cells $J_e$, with a nonempty set $\mathcal M_e$ of available mode labels on each cell. Suppose that along every path:* 1. *the sets $\mathcal M_e$ for cells containing that path are pairwise disjoint, and their total cardinality is at most $D_*$;* 2. *for distinct overlapping cells, every cross pair of mode atoms has normalized inner product on the smaller cell at most $D_*^{-200}$ in absolute value.* *Assume the same cross-pair bound for distinct sets on the same cell. Let $P_e$ be orthogonal projection onto the restrictions of $\{\psi_m:m\in\mathcal M_e\}$, with output supported on $J_e$. Then $$\sum_e\\|P_eF\\|^2\le C\\|F\\|^2$$ with an absolute constant.* *The cross-pair hypothesis holds if the sets have no shared or adjacent modes in the later PRE graph and $J_e$ are the associated pre-cells. It also holds for actual-use cells if the sets have no shared or adjacent modes in the later CURRENT graph.
  - Explanation: Projection rows onto separated mode families satisfy an absolute Bessel bound.
- **claim-lifted-row-compression** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-lifted-row-compression)): Fix a role. For each group, suppose a family of mode projection rows $P_e$, supported on cells $J_e$, has squared Bessel bound at most $G_0$. Suppose $J_e\subseteq u_e$, where $u_e$ is a usable main endpoint cell with a downstream actual-use certificate. The mode list of $P_e$ is drawn from the list available at $u_e$. Then the frozen row outputs $$P_e\,\mathscr R_{l,G}\, \overline L_{l,G,u_e}\,\mathcal B_{l,u_e}B_{l,u_e}$$ satisfy a joint squared bound $G_0\|R\|$, over all groups and rows. Here $B_{l,u_e}$ is the basic-$B$ input generated by its specified constituent main projection families from the original normalized input.
  - Explanation: Mode rows composed with the lift and shared histories keep a joint square bound.
- **claim-linear-side-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-linear-side)): For each basic-$B$ role, on every retained $v_b$-cell let $V_{l,G,b}$ be the exact subtotal of the linear-side modes in $\widetilde Z_{l,G,b}$, including their private coordinates. Set it to zero on branches without a needed output or certificate. Then $$\sum_{G,b}\\|V_{l,G,b}\\|^2\le G_0\|R\|.$$ In particular these functions satisfy Equation <a href="#eq:src-17" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-17">[eq:src-17]</a>, uniformly in the branch offsets.
  - Explanation: The linear-side subtotals have total square energy bounded by the overhead factor times the root length.
- **claim-hereditary-labels** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-hereditary-labels)): Fix the height, role, group, and type. We can choose a representative mode $a\in\mathcal E_{h-\Delta}$ for every successful component so that it is inherited whenever an earlier successful predecessor on the same path is contained in the current predecessor. Distinct representatives on comparable actual-use cells have no adjacent pair of component modes in the later CURRENT graph. In particular, if $I,I'$ are such cells and $x_*\in I\cap I'$, then their distinct representatives satisfy $$\left\|\omega_a(x_*)-\omega_{a'}(x_*) +n\cdot\vartheta/L_h\right\| >\frac{\Xi}{2} \max\bigl(\|I\|^{-1},\|I'\|^{-1}\bigr), \qquad n\in\mathbb Z^{\dim\theta_z},\quad \|n\|_\infty\le E_h . \tag{25}\label{eq:src-25}$$
  - Explanation: Representatives of successful components can be chosen hereditarily and are frequency separated on comparable cells.
- **claim-component-size** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-component-size)): At every actual cell, $$\begin{split} \sigma_{\mathcal F}^2 &\lesssim \\|P_{\mathcal F,I}\widetilde Z_{l,G,b}\\|_{2,I}^2 +\\|V_{\mathcal F}\\|_{2,I}^2+D_*^{-50},\\ \sum_{\mathcal F}\\|V_{\mathcal F}\\|_{2,I}^2 &\lesssim\\|V_{l,G,b}\\|_{2,I}^2 , \end{split} \tag{27}\label{eq:src-27}$$ where the second sum can be over the successful components. The same almost orthogonality controls arbitrary coefficient subtotals separated into distinct minimal CURRENT components.
  - Explanation: Component sizes are compared with projection norms and linear-side subtotals by almost orthogonality.
- **claim-top-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-top-count)): After removing summands on first stopping cells of arbitrarily small fixed total relative length, the following holds on every retained path. Fix a core role $j$, a height $h\in\mathcal H_{\mathrm{up}}$, a type, and a dyadic amplitude bin $\sigma_j\le\sigma_{\mathcal F}<2\sigma_j$. Let $\mathcal A_j^G$ be the distinct representatives of successful components of this type actually used in that bin along the path. Then $$\sum_G\|\mathcal A_j^G\| \le \min(D_*,P^C\sigma_j^{-2}). \tag{28}\label{eq:src-28}$$ The exponent $C$ is fixed. Enlarging $D_*$ by a fixed power is harmless in this statement. On retained uses, all component sizes and full local side sizes are at most $P^C$, and for a side role $$\sum_{s,G}\\|S_{l,G,s}\\|_{2,I_s}^2\le P^C.$$
  - Explanation: On retained paths the number of distinct representatives in an amplitude bin is bounded by a budget and an inverse square of the amplitude.
- **claim-sparse-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-sparse-count)): There are absolute constants $C$ and $C_{\mathrm{res}}$ with the following property. There are four roles $0,1,2,3$, of which either four or exactly three are designated core roles; write $J$ for their set. Let $M,L\geq1$, $d\geq0$, $B\geq1$, and $0<\mu\leq1\leq\nu$. In each core role $i$ let $\mathcal A_i$ be a finite set of labels, identified injectively with real numbers, with $\|\mathcal A_i\|\leq M$. Fix $\zeta\in\mathbb R^d$.* *For finitely many distinct integer indices $s$, let $R_s=R_0 2^s$ and let $\mathcal E_s\subseteq\prod_{i\in J}\mathcal A_i$ be a set of tuples; put $N_s=\|\mathcal E_s\|$. Assume the following.* 1. *Two labels in distinct core roles determine at most one full core tuple among all the $\mathcal E_s$, and the pair has at most $L$ scale occurrences.* 2. *If $l$ is a side role, there are $w_s\geq0$ with $\sum_s w_s\leq M$ such that, at scale $s$, the number of tuples incident to any fixed core label is at most $w_s$.* 3. *If distinct labels $a,a'$ in the same core role occur at scales $s,t$, respectively, then $$\|a-a'+n\cdot\zeta\|>\Sigma\max(R_s,R_t) \qquad(n\in\mathbb Z^d,\ \|n\|_\infty\leq Q).$$* 4. *For every triple $T\subseteq J$ of core roles, let $l'$ be the excluded role and set $\alpha_i=i-l'$ for $i\in T$. Every tuple at scale $s$ has, for this triple, a witness $n\in\mathbb Z^d$, $\|n\|_\infty\leq B$, such that $$\mu R_s\leq \left\|\sum_{i\in T}\alpha_i a_i+36n\cdot\zeta\right\| \leq\nu R_s.$$* 5. *At every occupied scale $s$, $$\|n\cdot\zeta\|\notin [\mu R_s/1000,1000\nu R_s] \qquad(n\in\mathbb Z^d,\ \|n\|_\infty\leq Q).$$* *Suppose that $$Q\geq C_{\mathrm{res}}(d+1)^2B, \qquad \Sigma\geq C_{\mathrm{res}}(d+1)^2\nu.$$ Then, with $\ell=1+\log_2(2+(d+1)^2\nu/\mu)$, one has $$\sum_{s:\ N_s\leq\delta M^2}N_s \leq C\ell L^{29}\delta^{1/2080}M^2 \qquad(0<\delta\leq1).$$ The constants and exponents are independent of $d$, the real frequencies, the number of scales, and all label cardinalities.
  - Explanation: The total mass of separated progression tests at scales of small density has a positive-power saving in the density.
- **claim-thin-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-thin-count)): There is an absolute $p_0\in(0,1)$ such that, for the preceding data and some $L_0\leq P^C(1+\log(\nu/\mu))^C$, one has $$\begin{split} \sum_sN_s^G &\leq L_0\min_{i\ne j}M_i^GM_j^G,\\ \sum_s(N_s^G)^{p_0} &\leq P^C(1+\nu+\mu^{-1}+\Lambda)^C(M')^{2p_0}. \end{split} \tag{34}\label{eq:src-34}$$ All exponents are absolute. They do not depend on the path, the group, the horizontal dimension, or the number of depths.
  - Explanation: Tested core tuple counts satisfy a pair-determination bound and a sublinear power bound.
- **claim-core-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-core-estimate)): There are absolute $\varepsilon>0$ and $C_4<\infty$, with $1-\varepsilon>2/3$, such that the terms containing at least three core roles in the localized expansion of <a href="#eq:src-11" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:src-11">[eq:src-11]</a> have total contribution at most $$C_q\eta^\varepsilon P^{C_4}\|R\|+O(e^{-k}\|R\|). \tag{37}\label{eq:src-37}$$ Here the original cell minimum is applied before the subadditive estimates, and all numerical errors are estimated absolutely. The constants are uniform in the finite depth range and the retained attempt. In particular $\varepsilon$ and $C_4$ can be fixed before choosing how close $q$ is to $2$.
  - Explanation: Terms with at least three core roles contribute a positive power of the pair-smallness parameter times a polynomial in the localization parameter, plus a negligible error.

        "exact_quote": "If $\\mathcal A$ is any set of columns available on a cell $I$, then $$D^{-1}\\left(\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal A}|c_v|^2\\right)^{1/2}\n \\le \\left\\|\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal A}c_v\\Phi_v(y)\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\n \\le D^{3/2}\\left(\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal A}|c_v|^2\\right)^{1/2}\n \\quad\\text{for almost every }y\\in I.$$ In particular, the coefficients are unique. The orthogonal projection $P_{I,\\mathcal A}$ onto their span satisfies $$\\|P_{I,\\mathcal A}z\\|_{L^\\infty(I;\\mathcal H)}\n       \\le D^5\\mathop{\\mathchoice{\\mkern 2mu\\int\\mkern-15mu-\\mkern 7mu}{\\mkern 2mu\\int\\mkern-13mu-\\mkern 6mu}{\\mkern 2mu\\int\\mkern-11mu-\\mkern 5mu}{\\mkern 2mu\\int\\mkern-9mu-\\mkern 4mu}}\\nolimits_I\\|z(y)\\|_{\\mathcal H}\\,dy.$$ For an actual scalar input $F=(f,0)$, write $P_{I,\\mathcal A}F=\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal A}c_v\\Phi_v$. Then $$\\mathop{\\mathchoice{\\mkern 2mu\\int\\mkern-15mu-\\mkern 7mu}{\\mkern 2mu\\int\\mkern-13mu-\\mkern 6mu}{\\mkern 2mu\\int\\mkern-11mu-\\mkern 5mu}{\\mkern 2mu\\int\\mkern-9mu-\\mkern 4mu}}\\nolimits_I\\bigl(f-(P_{I,\\mathcal A}F)_{\\mathrm{phys}}\\bigr)\n                         \\overline{\\varphi_v}\n       =\\epsilon_v c_v \\qquad(v\\in\\mathcal A).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-086.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-projection-prefixes",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal X$ be a Hilbert space, and for $1\\le i\\le m$ let $P_0^i,\\ldots,P_T^i$ be nested orthogonal projections on $\\mathcal X$. Set $Z_n=P_n^m\\cdots P_n^1$ and $$L_T=1+\\left\\lceil\\log_2(T+1)\\right\\rceil.$$ Suppose $B_\\nu:\\mathcal X\\to\\mathcal Y_\\nu$ are finitely many bounded linear operators satisfying $\\sum_\\nu\\|B_\\nu h\\|^2\\le\\|h\\|^2$. For any choices $n(\\nu)\\in\\{0,\\ldots,T\\}$, $$\\left(\\sum_\\nu\\|B_\\nu Z_{n(\\nu)}g\\|^2\\right)^{1/2}\n       \\le L_T^m\\|g\\|.$$ Moreover, $$\\sum_{n=1}^T\\|(Z_n-Z_{n-1})g\\|^2\n       \\le m^2L_T^{2(m-1)}\\|g\\|^2\n       \\le m^2L_T^{2m}\\|g\\|^2.$$ For $m=0$ the product is the identity and the first estimate has constant one.",
        "source_id": "PM-086.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-projection-prefixes",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal X$ be a Hilbert space, and for $1\\le i\\le m$ let $P_0^i,\\ldots,P_T^i$ be nested orthogonal projections on $\\mathcal X$. For any choices $n(\\nu)\\in\\{0,\\ldots,T\\}$, $$\\left(\\sum_\\nu\\|B_\\nu Z_{n(\\nu)}g\\|^2\\right)^{1/2}\n       \\le L_T^m\\|g\\|.$$ Moreover, $$\\sum_{n=1}^T\\|(Z_n-Z_{n-1})g\\|^2\n       \\le m^2L_T^{2(m-1)}\\|g\\|^2\n       \\le m^2L_T^{2m}\\|g\\|^2.$$ For $m=0$ the product is the identity and the first estimate has constant one.",
          "source_id": "PM-086.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-compression",
        "exact_quote": "Use the column system and budget $D\\ge2$ above on a finite dyadic tree with root $J$. Let $P^1,\\ldots,P^m$ be inherited mask families, with their local orthogonal projections. Suppose finitely many bounded linear rows $$B_\\nu:L^2(J;\\mathcal H)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal Y_\\nu$$ satisfy $$\\sum_\\nu\\|B_\\nu z\\|_{\\mathcal Y_\\nu}^2\\le\\|z\\|_{L^2(J;\\mathcal H)}^2,\n \\qquad B_\\nu=B_\\nu\\mathbf 1_{u(\\nu)},$$ where each $u(\\nu)$ is a cell of the tree. Then an absolute constant $C$ satisfies $$\\left(\\sum_\\nu\n    \\|B_\\nu P_{u(\\nu)}^m\\cdots P_{u(\\nu)}^1z\\|^2\\right)^{1/2}\n \\le \\bigl(C(m+1)^C\\log(2D)\\bigr)^{C(m+1)}\\|z\\|_{L^2(J;\\mathcal H)}.$$ The estimate is uniform in the row cells, the total number of labels, and the number of spatial depths. Identity factors may be omitted. Products containing complements $1-P^i$ satisfy the same form of bound after changing $C$. Integrating such compositions against a fixed finite measure costs, in addition, at most the total variation of that measure.",
        "source_id": "PM-086.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-compression",
          "exact_quote": "Use the column system and budget $D\\ge2$ above on a finite dyadic tree with root $J$. Then an absolute constant $C$ satisfies $$\\left(\\sum_\\nu\n    \\|B_\\nu P_{u(\\nu)}^m\\cdots P_{u(\\nu)}^1z\\|^2\\right)^{1/2}\n \\le \\bigl(C(m+1)^C\\log(2D)\\bigr)^{C(m+1)}\\|z\\|_{L^2(J;\\mathcal H)}.$$ The estimate is uniform in the row cells, the total number of labels, and the number of spatial depths. Identity factors may be omitted. Products containing complements $1-P^i$ satisfy the same form of bound after changing $C$. Integrating such compositions against a fixed finite measure costs, in addition, at most the total variation of that measure.",
          "source_id": "PM-086.1"

Then an absolute constant $C$ satisfies $$\\left(\\sum_\\nu\n    \\|B_\\nu P_{u(\\nu)}^m\\cdots P_{u(\\nu)}^1z\\|^2\\right)^{1/2}\n \\le \\bigl(C(m+1)^C\\log(2D)\\bigr)^{C(m+1)}\\|z\\|_{L^2(J;\\mathcal H)}.$$ The estimate is uniform in the row cells, the total number of labels, and the number of spatial depths.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-height-bins",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge1$, $B\\ge2$, and $\\xi\\in\\mathbb R^d$. Let $$\\mathcal E=\\left\\{|a\\cdot\\xi|:\n       a\\in\\mathbb Q^d\\text{ has height at most }B,\\ a\\cdot\\xi\\ne0\\right\\}.$$ There is an absolute constant $C$ such that the following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *The set $\\{\\log_2v:v\\in\\mathcal E\\}$ is contained in a union of at most $d$ intervals, each of length at most $C(d+1)^3\\log(2B)$. In particular, $\\mathcal E$ meets at most $C(d+1)^4\\log(2B)$ dyadic magnitude bins.*\n\n2.  *If $r_n=r_0 2^{-n}$ with $r_0>0$, $\\ell\\in\\{1,2\\}$, and $0<\\alpha\\le\\beta$, then $$\\#\\{n\\in\\mathbb Z:\\text{ some }v\\in\\mathcal E\n                      \\text{ satisfies }\\alpha\\le r_n^\\ell v\\le\\beta\\}\n     \\le C(d+1)^4\\bigl(\\log(2B)+\\log(2\\beta/\\alpha)\\bigr).$$*\n\n3.  *For every $\\varepsilon>0$, with the maximum of an empty set defined to be zero, $$\\sum_{n\\in\\mathbb Z}\n         \\max\\bigl(\\{r_n^\\ell v:v\\in\\mathcal E,\n                           r_n^\\ell v\\le\\varepsilon\\}\\cup\\{0\\}\\bigr)\n         \\le C(d+1)^4\\log(2B)\\,\\varepsilon.$$*\n\n*The same conclusions apply to suprema of finitely many such coordinate evaluations. For Euclidean norms of evaluation vectors with at most $h$ coordinates, one can add $C d\\log(2h)$ to the bounds for the number of occupied bins and scales, and to the coefficient of $\\varepsilon$ in the last estimate. No bound on the size of $\\xi$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-086.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

In particular, $\\mathcal E$ meets at most $C(d+1)^4\\log(2B)$ dyadic magnitude bins.*\n\n2.  *If $r_n=r_0 2^{-n}$ with $r_0>0$, $\\ell\\in\\{1,2\\}$, and $0<\\alpha\\le\\beta$, then $$\\#\\{n\\in\\mathbb Z:\\text{ some }v\\in\\mathcal E\n                      \\text{ satisfies }\\alpha\\le r_n^\\ell v\\le\\beta\\}\n     \\le C(d+1)^4\\bigl(\\log(2B)+\\log(2\\beta/\\alpha)\\bigr).$$*\n\n3.  *For every $\\varepsilon>0$, with the maximum of an empty set defined to be zero, $$\\sum_{n\\in\\mathbb Z}\n         \\max\\bigl(\\{r_n^\\ell v:v\\in\\mathcal E,\n                           r_n^\\ell v\\le\\varepsilon\\}\\cup\\{0\\}\\bigr)\n         \\le C(d+1)^4\\log(2B)\\,\\varepsilon.$$*\n\n*The same conclusions apply to suprema of finitely many such coordinate evaluations.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-weyl",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\vartheta$ be supported in a fixed box in $\\mathbb R^k$, with supremum norm and Lipschitz norm at most $H$. Suppose $0<\\delta<1/2$ and $$\\sup_{\\xi\\in\\mathbb R^k}\n \\left|\\int_{\\mathbb R^k}\\vartheta(z)Q(z)e(-\\xi^Tz)\\,dz\\right|\n \\ge\\delta .$$ There is a rational subspace $U\\subset\\mathbb R^d$, with orthogonal projections $P_f$ onto $U$ and $P_s=1-P_f$, such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n A|_{U\\times U}=0,\\qquad \\|V_s\\|\\le H',\\qquad\n \\|\\Lambda_*\\|\\le H',\\\\\n V_f=P_fV,\\quad V_s=P_sV,\\qquad\n \\Lambda_*=\\Lambda+\\tfrac12V^TC_{\\mathrm{sy}}V+\n                     \\operatorname{sym}(V_f^TC_{\\mathrm{sk}}V_s).\n \\end{gathered}\n \\tag{2}\\label{eq:src-2}$$ The heights of $P_f,P_s$ are at most $H'$, where $$H'\\le\n \\exp\\!\\left(C(1+\\log H+\\log(1/\\delta))^C\\right).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-086.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-weyl",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\vartheta$ be supported in a fixed box in $\\mathbb R^k$, with supremum norm and Lipschitz norm at most $H$. Suppose $0<\\delta<1/2$ and $$\\sup_{\\xi\\in\\mathbb R^k}\n \\left|\\int_{\\mathbb R^k}\\vartheta(z)Q(z)e(-\\xi^Tz)\\,dz\\right|\n \\ge\\delta .$$ There is a rational subspace $U\\subset\\mathbb R^d$, with orthogonal projections $P_f$ onto $U$ and $P_s=1-P_f$, such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n A|_{U\\times U}=0,\\qquad \\|V_s\\|\\le H',\\qquad\n \\|\\Lambda_*\\|\\le H',\\\\\n V_f=P_fV,\\quad V_s=P_sV,\\qquad\n \\Lambda_*=\\Lambda+\\tfrac12V^TC_{\\mathrm{sy}}V+\n                     \\operatorname{sym}(V_f^TC_{\\mathrm{sk}}V_s).\n \\end{gathered}\n \\tag{2}\\label{eq:src-2}$$ The heights of $P_f,P_s$ are at most $H'$, where $$H'\\le\n \\exp\\!\\left(C(1+\\log H+\\log(1/\\delta))^C\\right).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-086.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\vartheta$ be supported in a fixed box in $\\mathbb R^k$, with supremum norm and Lipschitz norm at most $H$. Suppose $0<\\delta<1/2$ and $$\\sup_{\\xi\\in\\mathbb R^k}\n \\left|\\int_{\\mathbb R^k}\\vartheta(z)Q(z)e(-\\xi^Tz)\\,dz\\right|\n \\ge\\delta .$$ There is a rational subspace $U\\subset\\mathbb R^d$, with orthogonal projections $P_f$ onto $U$ and $P_s=1-P_f$, such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n A|_{U\\times U}=0,\\qquad \\|V_s\\|\\le H',\\qquad\n \\|\\Lambda_*\\|\\le H',\\\\\n V_f=P_fV,\\quad V_s=P_sV,\\qquad\n \\Lambda_*=\\Lambda+\\tfrac12V^TC_{\\mathrm{sy}}V+\n                     \\operatorname{sym}(V_f^TC_{\\mathrm{sk}}V_s).\n \\end{gathered}\n \\tag{2}\\label{eq:src-2}$$ The heights of $P_f,P_s$ are at most $H'$, where $$H'\\le\n \\exp\\!\\left(C(1+\\log H+\\log(1/\\delta))^C\\right).$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-chart-expansion",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a rational orthogonal splitting satisfies <a href=\"#eq:src-2\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-2\">[eq:src-2]</a>, with heights and the indicated sizes at most $B$. On a fixed box in $\\mathbb R^k$ there is an exact absolutely convergent expansion $$Q(z)=e(z^T\\Lambda_*z+b^Tz)\n                \\sum_\\ell a_\\ell e(\\sigma_\\ell^TVz).\n \\tag{3}\\label{eq:src-3}$$ It consists of quasipolynomially many families of Fourier series in at most $2d$ integer indices. Their rational frequencies have height at most $D(1+\\|\\ell\\|)$, where $D$ is quasipolynomial in $H,B$. The total absolute coefficient sum is bounded by such a $D$.*\n\n*More quantitatively, for any fixed integer $J\\ge1$, one can choose a quasipolynomial $D_J$ such that every dyadic shell of indices $T\\le1+\\|\\ell\\|<2T$ has absolute coefficient sum at most $$D_J T^{-J(d+1)^2}.$$ For fixed underlying oscillation parameters, the same estimate, after enlarging $D_J$, holds for the coefficient envelope obtained by pooling all rational projection choices of height at most $B$, separately for each resulting frequency and effective quadratic part. The coefficient envelopes are uniform over amplitudes with the stated complexity bounds and over the bounded slow values entering their construction. In particular the pooled tail can be made at most $\\varepsilon$ by truncation at a size quasipolynomial in $H,B,\\varepsilon^{-1}$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ap-separation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\varphi_i,\\varphi_k$ be bounded atoms of complexity at most $H$ in two distinct slots $i,k$, and let $a,b$ denote the remaining slots. Their product has an exact absolutely convergent expansion in terms $$c\\,\\psi_a(y_a)\\psi_b(y_b)Q(t).\n \\tag{4}\\label{eq:src-4}$$ All three factors are bounded atoms independent of the interval scale. For some $D$ quasipolynomial in $H$, the terms can be arranged in dyadic shells $T\\ge1$ with total coefficient mass at most $DT^{-100}$. In a shell $T$, each factor has dimension at most $\\log D$, and complexity at most $(DT)^C$; unrestricted real linear phases are permitted as in Definition <a href=\"#def:quadratic-atom\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:quadratic-atom\">11</a>.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-single-scale-separation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a term in <a href=\"#eq:src-4\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-4\">[eq:src-4]</a>, without its coefficient, of complexity at most $H$. Let $\\zeta$ be a fixed nonnegative smooth function, equal to one on the range of $t/r$ allowed by a local kernel $w_I$, and supported on a larger fixed interval. Put $$A_r=\\sup_{\\xi\\in\\mathbb R}\n       \\left|\\int_\\mathbb RQ(ru)\\zeta(u)e(-\\xi u)\\,du\\right|,\n \\qquad r=|I|.$$ Its bilinear form norm in the remaining two $L^2(I)$ slots, with normalized interval norms, is at most $C A_r$.*\n\n*If $A_r\\ge\\delta>0$, then, for every $0<\\varepsilon<1/2$, the kernel of this bilinear form, extended by zero off its support, can be approximated uniformly on $I\\times I$, to error $\\varepsilon$, by a finite separated sum $\\sum_\\nu c_\\nu\\phi_{\\nu,a}(y_a)\\phi_{\\nu,b}(y_b)$. The $\\phi_{\\nu,e}$ are bounded atoms, and their complexities, their number, and $\\sum_\\nu|c_\\nu|$ are quasipolynomial in $H,\\delta^{-1},\\varepsilon^{-1}$.*\n\n*Before separating the smooth kernel, the retained terms have phases $\\lambda_*t^2+\\gamma t$, where $|\\lambda_*|r^2$ is quasipolynomially bounded. The quantities $\\lambda_*$ are bounded-height rational combinations of $\\lambda$ and the entries of $\\theta\\otimes\\theta$; each $\\gamma-\\beta$ is a bounded-height rational combination of $\\theta$. The height bounds are quasipolynomial in the same parameters.",
        "source_id": "PM-086.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-lss",
        "exact_quote": "For a $1$-bounded function $f:[L]\\to\\mathbb C$ with $\\|f\\|_{U^3[L]}\\ge\\delta$, there are a degree-two filtered nilmanifold $G/\\Gamma$, a polynomial sequence $g:\\mathbb Z\\to G$ adapted to its filtration, and a function $F:G/\\Gamma\\to\\mathbb C$ such that $$\\left|\\frac1L\\sum_{n=1}^L\n        f(n)\\overline{F(g(n)\\Gamma)}\\right|\\ge\\varepsilon.$$ The dimension is at most $C(1+\\log(1/\\delta))^{C}$, and the complexity of $G/\\Gamma$, the Lipschitz norm of $F$, and $\\varepsilon^{-1}$ are at most $\\exp(C(1+\\log(1/\\delta))^C)$. The nilmanifold has a rational Malcev basis adapted to the filtration, with integer second-kind coordinates for $\\Gamma$. The Lipschitz norm includes the supremum norm.",
        "source_id": "PM-086.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-lss",

        "exact_quote": "The quantity $\\|\\cdot\\|_{\\mathcal U}$ is a seminorm.         "exact_quote": "Fix $q>2$ and $C_1\\ge1$.         "exact_quote": "Consider one fixed term of <a href=\"#eq:src-6\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-6\">[eq:src-6]</a>, born on $J$. Suppose the nested predictor family in position $a$ contains $\\Phi_p$ on every use interval and on all its descendants. Other columns are allowed, subject to the projection budget $D_{\\mathrm h}$. If $A$ is sufficiently large, there is an absolute $\\theta_0>0$ such that, whenever $t\\ge12$ and $k=k_t$, every pair of full arguments in <a href=\"#eq:src-8\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-8\">[eq:src-8]</a> has bilinear form norm at most $e^{-2k^{\\theta_0}}$ against two normalized $L^2(I)$ inputs.       "exact_statement": "For each original vertex $v$ there is a fixed template family in every slot, consisting of bounded pure columns of normalized tag $v$ and unrestricted linear modulations, with the following property.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-localization",
        "exact_quote": "For an active pattern in <a href=\"#eq:src-11\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-11\">[eq:src-11]</a>, there are $\\operatorname{poly}(k)$ fixed search leaves such that, up to total absolute length-weighted error at most $Ce^{-k}|R|$, each leaf contributes $$\\sum_{G\\ \\mathrm{current}}\n H_I(Z_{0,G,s},Z_{1,G,s},Z_{2,G,s},Z_{3,G,s}),\\qquad\n Z_{l,G,s}=\\overline L_{l,G,s}\\mathcal B_{l,s}z_l.\n \\tag{14}\\label{eq:src-14}$$ Here $\\overline L_{l,G,s}$ is one positive smeared ball filter, and $\\mathcal B_{l,s}$ is a product of cumulative filters or complements of length $O(\\log\\mathcal L)$, independent of $G$. The product and slot choices are fixed by the search leaf, uniformly over cells. A group is summed only at an actual current central use. The minimum with $\\eta$ in <a href=\"#eq:src-11\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-11\">[eq:src-11]</a> may be applied separately per leaf.",
        "source_id": "PM-086.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "At a use of <a href=\"#eq:src-14\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-14\">[eq:src-14]</a>, suppose slots $a,b$ are fixed side states $S_a,S_b$, chosen independently of the helper catalog. Suppose the other two slots have whole arguments $Z_{c,G,s},Z_{d,G,s}$ with basic inputs $B_c,B_d$. Expand every factor $1-\\overline Q$ in slots $c,d$ as the difference of the identity and a positive average. In each resulting term, all positive filters on these two slots can be removed, one at a time, by transferring corresponding helper averages to slot $a$. The main term at the end is $$H_I(\\mathcal D_{a,s}S_a,S_b,B_c,B_d),$$ where $\\mathcal D_{a,s}$ is a product of helper averages of length $O(\\log\\mathcal L)$.*\n\n\n*For a step transferring a filter from $c$ to $a$, let $Q_{c,p}$ be its individual outgoing projections, $1\\le p\\le P$, and let $Q_{a,p}$ be the helper-rank projections with the same masks and pads. For $l=a,c$, put $$Q_{l,0}=0,\\qquad Q_{l,P+1}=1,\\qquad\n A_l^p=Q_{l,p}-Q_{l,p-1},\\qquad\n \\overline Q_l=P^{-1}\\sum_{p=1}^P Q_{l,p}.$$ Let $x_c$ be the inner history on $B_c$ after deleting the outgoing filter. In the other slots, let $x_a=\\mathcal D_{a,s}S_a$ use the helper averages already transferred, let $x_b=S_b$, and let $x_d$ be the whole remaining history on $B_d$. The exact difference $$H_I(x_a,x_b,\\overline Q_c x_c,x_d)\n -H_I(\\overline Q_a x_a,x_b,x_c,x_d)$$ is $$\\sum_{p,q=1}^{P+1}\\frac{p-q}{P}\n H_I(A_a^p x_a,x_b,A_c^q x_c,x_d).\n \\tag{15}\\label{eq:src-15}$$ Only $|p-q|\\le2$ need be retained. The discarded far terms at each cell and group have total absolute size at most $D^{-C_3}\\lVert S_a\\rVert_{2,I}\\lVert S_b\\rVert_{2,I}$, for any prescribed fixed $C_3$, after the indicated choices of numerical accuracies.",
        "source_id": "PM-086.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sides-mixed-maximal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\mu)$ and $(W,\\nu)$ be finite measure spaces. If $1<r,s,t<\\infty$ and $v\\in A_r(\\mathbb R)$, then $$\\|M_y g\\|_{L_y^r(v;L_\\xi^s(X;L_w^t(W)))}\n \\le C_{r,s,t}([v]_{A_r})\n       \\|g\\|_{L_y^r(v;L_\\xi^s(X;L_w^t(W)))}.$$ The constant is independent of the numbers of atoms in the measure spaces. In particular, for finite exponents $p,b,h>q\\geq1$, $$\\|M_q f\\|_{L_y^pL_\\xi^bL_w^h}\n \\le C_{p,b,h,q}\\|f\\|_{L_y^pL_\\xi^bL_w^h}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-086.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sides-mixed-maximal",

In particular, for finite exponents $p,b,h>q\\geq1$, $$\\|M_q f\\|_{L_y^pL_\\xi^bL_w^h}\n \\le C_{p,b,h,q}\\|f\\|_{L_y^pL_\\xi^bL_w^h}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-086.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-short-block",
        "exact_quote": "Let $2<q<3$. Let $\\mathcal T$ be a finite dyadic tree piece with root $R$, whose internal summation intervals belong to at most $K$ consecutive depths and to the original range of at most $N$ depths. Suppose its leaves occur in the same depth block or one depth below it. In slot $j\\in\\{0,1,2,3\\}$ let $\\mathbf z_j$ be a deterministic Hilbert-space-valued function supported on $R$, with $$\\|\\mathbf z_j\\|_{2,L}\\le s_j\n \\qquad\\text{for every leaf }L.$$ Let $z_j^\\sigma$ be random scalar inputs such that, for every fixed finite $a\\geq1$ used below, $$\\bigl(\\mathbb E_\\sigma|z_j^\\sigma(y)|^a\\bigr)^{1/a}\n \\le C_a\\|\\mathbf z_j(y)\\|.$$ The constants $C_a$ are independent of the tree and $K$; products of at most two independent Rademacher randomizations of a vector stack have this property. For arbitrary measurable kernel signs $|\\epsilon_I(\\sigma)|\\le1$, and any subcollection of the internal intervals, $$\\mathbb E_\\sigma\\left|\n   \\sum_I |I|\\epsilon_I(\\sigma)\n             H_I(z_0^\\sigma,z_1^\\sigma,z_2^\\sigma,z_3^\\sigma)\n             \\right|\n \\le (1+C_N(q))C_q\n       (1+\\log(K+2))^C(K+2)^{C(q-2)}\n       |R|\\prod_{j=0}^3s_j .\n \\tag{16}\\label{eq:src-16}$$ Here $C$ is absolute. The constant $C_q$ may depend on the fixed moment bounds, as well as the fixed kernel bounds.",
        "source_id": "PM-086.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $Z_{G,s}$ be a basic coarse output in Equation <a href=\"#eq:src-14\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-14\">[eq:src-14]</a>. At each right endpoint, restrict to a union of $t$-cells having a downstream actual use certificate for $G$. Then $$\\sum_{G,[r,t]}\\|\n  \\mathbf 1_{E_{G,r,t}}(Z_{G,t}-Z_{G,r})\\|_2^2\n \\le G_0\\|\\mathbf 1_RF\\|_2^2.$$ The same conclusion holds for a shared coarse history with no outer ball, omitting the group sum.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-sides-stacks",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a near offset $q-p$ in Equation <a href=\"#eq:src-15\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-15\">[eq:src-15]</a>, a transfer step, and a search path. The two time-dependent side inputs can be written, at their uses, as a sum of at most small-overhead many configurations of fixed vector stacks for each $(G,b)$. One position is selected at a use from each stack. The banded side stack also includes the band index. After absorbing the number of configurations in $G_0$, their energies satisfy $$\\sum_{G,b}\\|\\mathrm{stack}_{a,G,b}\\|_2^2\n       \\le P G_0|R|,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{G,b}\\|\\mathrm{stack}_{\\mathrm{untouched},G,b}\\|_2^2\n       \\le G_0|R|.\n \\tag{18}\\label{eq:src-18}$$ The functions in these stacks are fixed before subsequent cuts of the summation set.",

      },

This includes a fourth side, a bounded current argument, or a bounded current argument minus side factors.*\n\n*Terms having exactly two side factors and whole current coarse complements admit the following estimates. Their freeze errors, large-complement terms, and restoration errors have total length-weighted $p$th-power cost at most $P^C|R|$. Their transferred main terms cost at most $\\eta P^C|R|$ absolutely. Their numerical errors are negligible after the stated choice of the main precision. Their remaining coupled near-band terms obey Equation <a href=\"#eq:src-19\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:src-19\">[eq:src-19]</a> without taking a power less than one.*\n\n*All statements are uniform on normalized subroots with the same basic data.
